PREFACE

Since this study is a continuation

67 67712
of the author's earlier com-

Spilation , The Parisian Stage : Part I ( 1800-1815 ), reference is here

made to the preface to that volume . Once again the aim is to supply an authoritative reference work in a field that lacks any such

work . For the benefit of those readers who do not have access to

Part I , a few explanatory remarks may be repeated concerning the

of the study and the methods used .
This is intended as the most complete list available in print of
the dramatic productions presented in Paris in the early nineteenth

purpose

The period covered in this volume is 1816 through 1830 .
primary
The
source of information has been the announcements of

century .

the theaters in the contemporary newspapers . This source has been
supplemented by use of specialized theatrical journals , compilations ,
and studies.1
Considerable effort has been made to check and

verify details such as titles , dates , and theaters , and especially to
identify anonymous and pseudonymous authors . It is believed that
Part II will exceed the standard of accuracy achieved in Part I. The
author hopes in the future to carry this study forward at least to
1850 .

As in Part I , this study is divided into two main sections . The
Table des Pièces , attempts to present a complete and acalphabetical
curate
list of new French plays of all types performed
regular
in the
theaters of Paris from January 1, 1816 , to December
31 , 1830.2 The listing is by title , ³ which is followed by indications
of as many of the following details as are known :
sub - title
type of play
number of acts

first , the

whether in verse or prose
real names of authors¹
theater of first performance in Paris
date of first performance in Paris5
1 Cf. Bibliography for list of reference works most frequently consulted .
2 Not listed are translations of foreign plays and the Italian opera bouffa , since
these are not considered
to be essentially French plays .
3 Contrary to frequent French practice , the initial indefinite article is disregarded
in alphabetization except when used as a pronoun .
4 It is felt that it is more helpful to have the real names in these entries , even
though other sources may give the pseudonym .
5 Dates are noted in the French style : day month year . Thus , 11-1-08 means
11 janvier 1808. The same system is used for the republican calendar : 11
vent . VIII means 11 ventose , An
.

When a play has also been known by its sub - title , this is listed separately , with cross - reference to the main entry . In the cases of
opéras and opéras - comiques , the parenthetical
de . . . )" indicates the composer of the music .
system is consecutive from the end of Part I.

notation " (mus .
The numbering

The second section consists of a cumulative Table des Auteurs ,
, with reference to the numbered items in
the Tables des Pièces of both volumes . It is hoped that this cumulative feature will increase the usefulness of the work as a whole .

arranged alphabetically

or alphabetization of authors '
names , the usage of the Catalogue of the Bibliothèque Nationale is
followed . Pseudonyms are listed with reference to the real names .
Names of composers of music are followed by the indication

In cases of doubt

as to the spelling

"(mus .)"

A table of corrections and additions to Part I has been inserted ,

keyed to the numbered items in that volume . " Where omittted
plays have been located for the period 1800-1815 , they are included

in appropriate order here . Such new plays from that period are
also listed , for indexing purposes , as items 3019-3030 at the beginning of the Table des Pièces of this Part II . In the Table des Auteurs these plays are listed under their authors in their appropriate
numerical order , with a parenthetical reference to the number under which they appear at the beginning of this volume .
The author takes pleasure in recording here his gratitude to
the Research Committee of the University of Alabama for a grantin- aid which enabled him to undertake this project .

6 No effort has been made to list the composers of music for mélodrames .

to some of the changes . The suggestions of Professors Girdler B. Fitch and
Walter Staaks have been particularly helpful . Suggestions for corrections or
additions to Part
will be welcomed .
